美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險
2026 年 11 月 04 日
一、 市場動態
1. 美期中選舉牽動市場情緒，亞股一度隨美股期貨下挫：美國 11 月 3 日舉行期中選舉
，根據 CNN 網站的結果顯示，截至台北時間 11/4 下午 3 點左右，民主黨於參議院
已取得 100 席中的 53 席的過半席次，於眾議院則在 435 席中取得了 225 席(218 席
過半)。市場重新評估國會控制權及川普政府未來政策推動能力，擔心民主黨國會影響
力擴大，可能對財政政策及科技企業監管形成新的制衡。S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100

期貨在開票期間一度下挫 1.38%及 2.35%，科技股明顯承受較大賣壓。惟隨後跌深反
彈，截至台北時間下午 3 時左右，S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100 期貨跌幅已收斂至
0.22%及 0.44%。亞洲市場方面，科技股為主及出口導向股市受到美股期貨拖累，台
灣加權指數 11/4 盤中最低下挫 3.38%，所幸隨跌幅收斂，終場下跌 0.83%；韓股同
日也由大跌近 4%由黑翻紅上漲 0.62%。11/3 未開盤的日股，在 11/4 開盤後出現補
跌，最低下挫近 4.8%，但最後跌幅收斂至下跌 1.42%。
2. 美國公債及美元同步反映政策預期調整，市場避險情緒升溫：美國公債及美元反映政
策預期調整，殖利率盤中先跌後漲：10 年期公債殖利率盤中先跌後漲，截至台北時間
下午 3 時來到 4.906%；同一時間，美元指數小跌約 0.37%。股、債、匯市的變動顯
示，投資人除了調整股票風險部位，也在評估未來美國財政刺激規模、政府債務供給
與利率前景。
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨取得國會控制權，市場重新定價財政與監管政策：本次選舉對金融市場的主要

影響，在於未來行政與立法部門的權力分配可能改變。共和黨先前同時掌握白宮與國
會，有利於推動稅制、財政及產業政策；民主黨在參眾兩院均取得過半席次後，部分

政策將面臨更高的協商成本。財政方面，新增減稅及擴大支出的空間可能受到限制，
市場因而下修部分財政刺激預期；企業方面，國會監督權可能增加對大型科技企業、
AI 資料中心、反壟斷及資料隱私議題的調查壓力。惟國會控制權改變不代表既有政策
立即逆轉，也不代表所有監管措施均能完成立法。此次市場反應更可能是投資人重新
要求政策風險溢酬，而非企業獲利已發生實質下修。
2. AI 資本支出與投資回報疑慮尚未消退，放大科技股對政治事件的反應：此次選舉開票
期間，Nasdaq 期貨及與科技相關的亞股出現大幅波動，亦與近期科技股基本面及評

價爭議有關。10 月底以來美股因大型科技企業 AI 投資效益受到質疑而大幅修正，市
場關注焦點逐漸由 AI 基礎建設支出規模，轉向相關投資能否創造足夠的營運現金流與
股東報酬。以雲端服務及資料中心產業而言，企業雖可透過 AI 運算需求增加帶動營收
成長，但 GPU、伺服器、電力設備及資料中心建設需要龐大前期資金，若營收回收速
度不如預期，可能使自由現金流承壓並增加外部融資需求。近期部分科技企業籌資計
畫及財測不佳，已使投資人提高對財務品質的要求。當市場原先已對 AI 投資回報存有
疑慮，新的政治及監管不確定性便更容易放大評價修正幅度。
三、 市場看法
1. 半導體需求仍具分化，政治事件不等於 AI 產業成長趨勢反轉：科技股近期的股價修正
與 AI 終端需求惡化並非相同概念。AMD 近期公布優於市場預期的財報，並對資料中
心業務提出較強勁的成長展望，顯示 AI 運算需求仍具有一定韌性；相對而言，Intel
先前財測疲弱及現金流壓力，則反映不同半導體企業的產品競爭力、資本效率及終端
市場曝險存在明顯差異。因此，不宜將半導體股整體下跌解讀為 AI 需求全面降溫。從

產業鏈觀察，具備技術領先優勢、穩定客戶需求及良好現金流的企業，與高度依賴外
部融資、尚未形成穩定獲利模式的基礎建設業者，對資金成本及監管變化的承受能力
並不相同。未來市場可能更加重視個別企業的獲利品質，而非單純以 AI 相關營收成長
作為估值依據。
2. 政治事件可能延長科技股評價調整，但中期走勢仍取決於企業獲利：本次期中選舉引
發的期貨跌勢，反映市場對政策方向及國會控制權變化的即時反應，惟政治不確定性
通常須透過實際立法、企業投資計畫或經濟活動變化，才會形成較持久的基本面影響
。從企業獲利角度觀察，路透社引用 LSEG 資料指出，S&P 500 第三季企業獲利仍呈
現強勁成長，科技企業對整體獲利貢獻顯著，顯示目前尚無充分證據支持美國企業基
本面全面轉弱。不過，科技股先前已累積較高成長預期，市場對資本支出回報及監管
風險的容忍度降低，可能使股價即使在營收成長下仍承受評價壓力。短期而言，若選
舉結果逐漸明朗，且企業獲利預估未進一步下修，市場有機會逐步消化政治風險；反
之，若政策不確定性伴隨獲利預期下修，修正時間可能延長。

不另行通知。
3. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：根據彭博統計，1950
年以來美國歷次期中選舉後 12 個月，S&P 500 平均報酬率約 15%，且 19 次全數
收正。其中最高為 1954 年的逾 30%，表現最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。我們認為
，選前政策不確定性消除加上府會分治抑制財政與監管的轉向是為歷史上選後行情的
主要支撐，但相關數據所呈現的為過往經驗，未來股市表現能否延續先前情況仍應觀
察後續基本面變化。
4. 美國公債具避險價值，但利率下行能否持續仍取決於通膨與財政前景：在美國期中選

舉開票期間，美股期貨下跌伴隨美債殖利率回落，顯示公債仍能在股市波動期間提供
一定的風險分散效果。然而，本次殖利率下降可能同時包含避險買盤、財政刺激預期
下修及市場對經濟成長前景重新評估等因素，不宜直接解讀為聯準會即將轉向寬鬆。
尤其能源市場仍受到中東地緣政治及供應中斷風險影響，油價波動可能使通膨前景反
覆，限制長天期公債殖利率的下行空間。若未來財政擴張受到限制，可能對長端利率
形成部分支撐；但若政治僵局提高債務上限協商風險，亦可能使美債期限溢酬波動。
因此，債券配置仍須同時考量收益率、存續期間與通膨風險，不宜僅根據單日行情決
定配置方向。
5. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：目前科技股修正主
要反映評價與政策風險，但若 AI 企業持續增加資本支出及外部融資，市場對債務負擔
與償債能力的關注可能進一步升高。對大型投資等級科技企業而言，較穩定的營運現
金流及融資管道有助於吸收資本支出壓力；但對依賴單一客戶合約、設備抵押融資或
高槓桿經營的 AI 基礎建設業者，利率與信用利差上升可能更直接影響投資回報及再融

資能力。後續應追蹤投資等級債與高收益債 OAS、科技及雲端相關發行人利差、新債
發行需求及信用評等展望。若科技股下跌但整體信用利差維持穩定，市場風險可能仍
以股權評價調整為主；若信用利差同步持續擴大，則須提高對金融條件收緊及風險跨
市場擴散的警覺。

四、 投資建議：
1. 科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，惟短線宜審慎布局並重視個股分化：美國期中選舉帶
來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建
設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，目前尚不宜因美股期貨在開票期間的
下挫即全面轉向保守。我們仍建議維持美股核心配置，但短期避免過度集中於高評價
、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企業。產業方面，資訊科技維持選擇性配置，優
先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業；對雲端基
礎建設及 AI 融資相關標的，則應提高財務結構與現金流審查標準。

2. 維持中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債配置，兼顧收益與風險分散：在美國政策風
險升溫及科技股評價調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源
價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延長存續期間。建議以中天期美國公債及高信評投資
等級債作為主要配置方向，兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置穩定性。信用債方
面，建議優先選擇營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人，對 AI 相關
企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

——2026年 10月物价数据点评
[Table_Industry]日期: shzqdatemark [Table_Summary] 主要观点
价格走势分化，PPI 涨幅扩大
本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，而上涨项
仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月
扩大 5.8个百分点，影响 CPI同比上涨约 0.63个百分点，带动能源价
格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除食品和能源价格的核
心 CPI 同比上涨 1.0%，小幅回升，基本保持稳定。 PPI 同比上涨
5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大 0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭
开采、石油开采、石油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金
属矿采选、有色金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9个行业合计影响 PPI 同
比上涨约 6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。

1 事件：10月 CPI、PPI数据公布
国家统计局数据显示，2026年 10月份，全国居民消费价格同
比上涨 0.7%。其中，城市上涨 0.7%，农村上涨 0.7%；食品价格
下降 2.9%，非食品价格上涨 1.5%；消费品价格上涨 0.8%，服务
价格上涨 0.7%。1—10月平均，全国居民消费价格比上年同期上
涨 0.9%。10月份，全国居民消费价格环比持平。其中，城市持平，
农村下降 0.1%；食品价格下降 1.0%，非食品价格上涨 0.2%；消
费品价格下降 0.1%，服务价格上涨 0.1%。
2026年 10月份，全国工业生产者出厂价格同比上涨 5.7%，
环比上涨 0.7%。工业生产者购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，环比上涨
1.1%。1—10月平均，工业生产者出厂价格比上年同期上涨 2.7%，
工业生产者购进价格上涨 4.0%。
表 1：10月 CPI、PPI同比数据（%）
资料来源：IFIND，上海证券研究所
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因

2.1 CPI小幅回落
本月全国 CPI环比由上月上涨 0.2%转为持平。食品价格由上
月上涨 0.4%转为下降 1.0%，影响 CPI环比下降约 0.17个百分点，
是 CPI 环比下降的主要原因。其中，猪肉、鲜菜、鸡蛋、鲜果、
水产品价格均有不同程度的下降。非食品方面，受国际市场价格
变动影响，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.7 个百分
点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.14个百分点；服务方面，服务价格由
上月下降 0.2%转为上涨 0.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04个百分
点，其中受国庆长假的刺激，相关旅游出行等服务价格有所上涨。
CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2 个百分点。具体来
看，食品价格下降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，影响
CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点。食品中，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果价格
分别下降 16.9%、8.6%和 3.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.54
个百分点。非食品方面，受汽油涨价影响，能源价格涨幅扩大。

汽油价格上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI

本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，
而上涨项仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分
点，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除
食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2
个百分点，基本保持稳定。PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大
0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭开采、石油开采、石
油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金属矿采选、有色
金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9 个行业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约
6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。
4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 推动经济向新向优向好发展
10月价格持续分化，CPI走势偏稳，PPI持续上涨。CPI同比
的变动越来越受到非食品的影响，尤其是汽油价格，但由于食品
项偏弱的表现，CPI 整体平稳，核心 CPI 也保持稳定。工业品价

格同比涨幅有所扩大，上游原材料与下游制造业均有所涨价，尤
其是人工智能持续赋能产业转型带动计算机通信、电气机械等行
业上涨突出。政策方面，7月政治局会议提出要稳定生猪等农畜产
品生产和价格，实施好更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政
策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量
政策，加大逆周期调节力度，推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，
努力实现“十五五”良好开局。
5 风险提示：
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变
化。

このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全

2026年 11月 19日 全9頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月機械受注 
製造業が堅調で7-9月期の民需（船電除く）は4四半期連続で増加

経済調査部 エコノミスト 吉井 希祐

[要約] 
◼ 2026年9月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比▲0.3％と2カ月ぶりに減少し、コン
センサス（Bloomberg調査：同＋1.5％）を下回った。民需（船電除く）を 3カ月移動
平均で見ると同＋0.3％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみ
られる」に据え置いた。 
◼ 製造業からの受注額は前月比＋3.9％と 2カ月連続で増加した。化学工業では大型案件
が 1件あり、全体を押し上げた。非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は同＋1.1％と 2
カ月連続で増加した。運輸業・郵便業や情報サービス業では大型案件が 1 件ずつあり、
全体を大きく押し上げた。 
◼ 先行きの民需（船電除く）は緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。企業の設備投資意
欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待され
よう。ただし、中東情勢が悪化すれば、事業環境の不確実性の高まりやエネルギー・
原材料価格の一段の高騰への懸念から、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れが
ある。

【総括】船電除く民需は小幅に減少したものの、製造業・非製造業はいずれも増加 
2026年 9月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比▲0.3％と 2カ月ぶりに減少し、コンセン
サス（Bloomberg調査：同＋1.5％）を下回った（図表2）。製造業は2カ月連続で増加し、非製
造業（船電除く）も 2カ月連続で増加した 0F
1（図表 3）。民需（船電除く）を 3カ月移動平均で
見ると同＋0.3％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみられる」に据
え置いた。 
2026年7-9月期の民需（船電除く）は前期比＋1.2％と4四半期連続で増加したものの、6月
末時点の企業見通し（同＋4.9％）には届かなかった。製造業は同＋8.0％と 2四半期ぶりに増
加した。業種別で見ると、電気機械（同＋31.4％）が全体を大きく押し上げた。他方、非製造
業（船電除く）は同▲0.8％と2四半期連続で減少した。

【製造業】大型案件があった化学機械を中心に増加 
2026年 9月の製造業からの受注額は前月比＋3.9％と 2カ月連続で増加した。機種別に見る
と、産業機械、電子・通信機械、工作機械、輸送機械が増加した一方、原動機・重電機は減少
した（図表 4左、大和総研による季節調整値）。業種別では、17業種中 10業種が増加した。化
学工業（同＋57.0％）では化学機械の大型案件が 1 件あり、全体を押し上げた。業務用機械
（同＋80.5％）、その他製造業（同＋24.6％）などからの受注も増加した。他方、はん用・生産
用機械（同▲14.8％）や非鉄金属（同▲49.0％）などからの受注は減少した。

【非製造業】大型案件があった運輸業・郵便業や情報サービス業が大きく押し上げる 
2026年9月の非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は前月比＋1.1％と2カ月連続で増加した。
機種別に見ると、輸送機械、電子・通信機械、工作機械が増加した一方、産業機械、原動機・
重電機は減少した（図表 4右、大和総研による季節調整値）。業種別では、11業種中 3業種が
増加した。運輸業・郵便業（同＋51.3％）では鉄道車両、情報サービス業（同＋25.0％）では
通信機の大型案件が 1件ずつあり、全体を大きく押し上げた。他方、リース業（同▲49.4％）
や農林漁業（同▲16.5％）をはじめ、幅広い業種からの受注が減少した。

図表 4：業種別・機種別に見た機械受注額の動き

除外したが、26年9月は前月比＋10.3％であった。

【外需】9件の大型案件が下支えし、3カ月ぶりに増加 
2026年 9月の外需は前月比＋6.2％と 3カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 5）。機種別では、電子・
通信機械、原動機・重電機、産業機械、工作機械が増加した一方、輸送機械は減少した（図表
6、大和総研による季節調整値）。電子・通信機械で 3件、原動機・重電機で 2件、輸送機械で
4件の合計9件の大型案件があった。 
機械受注の外需動向を地域別に見る上で参考になる工作機械受注を確認すると、9月の外需
は前月比＋2.6％と4カ月連続で増加した（日本工作機械工業会、大和総研による季節調整値、
図表 7）。 アジア（中国を除く、同＋13.9％）からの受注が好調で 1F
2、全体を押し上げた。他方、
欧州（EU＋英国、同▲15.2％）と米国（同▲3.0％）からの受注は 4 カ月ぶりに減少し、中国
（同▲0.6％）からの受注は 2カ月ぶりに減少した（図表8）。  
工作機械受注は 10月分がすでに公表されており、内需は前月比＋2.2％と 2カ月ぶりに増加
した一方、外需は同▲5.8％と5カ月ぶりに減少した。

5 / 9

【先行き】民需（船電除く）の緩やかな増加基調を見込むも中東情勢等が下振れリスク 
先行きの民需（船電除く）は、緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。日銀短観の 9月調査に
おける「生産・営業用設備判断 DI」（先行き、全規模）を見ると、製造業（▲6％pt）と非製造
業（▲7％pt）のいずれでも、設備の不足感が示されている。企業の設備投資意欲は強く、人
手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI関連投資などが引き続き期待されよう。 
内閣府が公表した 2026年 10-12月期の見通しによれば、民需（船電除く）は前期比＋4.3％
と 5四半期連続での増加が見込まれており、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強いとみられる。
製造業は同＋2.6％、非製造業（船電除く）は同＋5.2％と、いずれも堅調に推移する見込みだ。 
AI関連投資に関しては、米国のハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる企業群の巨額の設備投資など
を背景に、関連製品を生産・輸出するはん用・生産用機械 2F
3や電気機械、情報通信機械などか
らの受注が増加傾向にあり、今後もこれらの業種からの受注が民需全体を押し上げるとみられ
る（図表 9）。ただし、米国では一部の AI関連企業の収益性や財務面への懸念に加え、中間選
挙の結果を受け、AIやデータセンターに対する規制強化への懸念が強まっている。こうした懸
念が現実化し、AI関連需要をけん引するハイパースケーラーの設備投資の増勢が大きく鈍化す
れば、関連する日本企業の設備投資が下振れする可能性があり、注意が必要だ 3F
4。 
中東情勢をめぐっては、ホルムズ海峡の事実上の封鎖が続くなど、依然として不透明感の強
い状況にある。情勢が悪化すれば、事業環境の不確実性の高まりやエネルギー・原材料価格の
一段の高騰への懸念から、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れがある 4F
5。 
図表 9：業種別の製造業の機械受注額への寄与度

（注）製造業の受注額（季節調整値の 3カ月移動平均）の2025年1月からの変化率に対する累積寄与度。 
（出所）内閣府統計より大和総研作成

パースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

影響」（大和総研レポート、2026年 11月17日）を参照。

## Document 105: 2347聯強 台新

Source PDF: all report pdf/2347聯強 台新.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本刊載之報告為台新投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效
性，審慎考量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬台新投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。 
台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

公司拜訪快報 
YoY+273%，半導體業務為營運成長另一重點業務，但因資金成本上揚，公司將
摒棄部分利潤未達目標專案，此將影響短期營收動能。 
受惠於出貨 ASP 增加，以及原廠擴大促銷力道挽救銷售量，3Q26 資訊家用和
手機業務分別+21%YoY 和+33%YoY。記憶體大漲致使原廠逐步反應成本於 PC
和手機新機售價，並將導致消費者延長換機週期，2027 年此兩塊業務成長性仍
相對保守看待。

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

Update
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Continuing capacity build with unchanged 2027 demand: The company mentioned
that AI demand remains strong and customers (especially the largest customer) are
still asking for more clean room build. 2027 demand is almost confirmed with no
changes over the past month.
Capacity allocation: US capacity will fully support the largest customer initially and
the capacity in Taiwan is fungible between different customers. For example, given

4Q26 weakness for consumer/smartphones, the company has shifted spare capacity
to meet other demand, including the testers from Advantest and Teradyne.
Margin should still be in the upward trend: The priority for the company now is to
have customers consign the equipment if they are asking for more capacity, lowering
the deprecation pressure. Also, given the tight capacity, the company would serve
the products with higher margin (generally AI demand and longer test time product)

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd (2449.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8%, intermediate-term
growth rate of 10.5%, terminal growth rate of 3.0%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 65%.
Risks to Upside
n Rise in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek
n Strong AI demand from both GPU and ASIC
n Penetration of wafer sorting business
n Rise in IDM outsourcing to OSAT

Risks to Downside
n Overall non-AI demand remaining weak
n Significant testing market share loss
n Drop in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

「逢低買進」，目標價: $3,300

報告日期：2026.11.16 研究員：陳泂暉

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/13 收盤價： 1,520.00 毛利重返成長軌道，上調投資評等至「買進」 
目前股價 12X27(F)PER，考量： 
(1) 26Q3 公 司 營 收 為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6% 、
YoY+51.3%，受惠 HPC 電源需求、SPE 沉積蝕刻及次
系統提升與電氣化推進；毛利率為 32.6% ，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，費用率創低帶動營益率創
高；稅後淨利 42.78億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；
稅後 EPS 21.93元。 
(2) 工業業務受惠工廠自動化回溫及能源、電動車等電氣化
需求推進，機器人、航太與醫療等客製化方案亦逐步貢
獻。半導體設備受惠沉積與蝕刻滲透率提升，客戶投資
看至 2030年後，帶動子系統整合與機箱組裝委外需求擴
大。 
(3) HPC受惠 AI基礎設施升級，機櫃內銅纜與機櫃外光纖將
長期共存。公司透過 XFS 強化光學封裝與量產，推進
CPO、12.8T XPO 及 Micro-VCSEL 技術；電源延伸至
Busbars、匯流排槽及水冷板等全液冷機櫃方案，結合
Interplex 機構整合並共同開發 LVDC/HVDC 架構，推升
系統級競爭力。 
(4) 預估 2027 年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於
新世代平台放量；毛利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於
產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，YoY+74.6%；稅
後 EPS 131.58元。

貿聯目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，位於公司過往 9-34X區間
下緣位置，考量公司持續推進光通業務，將有助於評價回升，
所以取 17X，給予「買進」投資評等， 6 個月目標價
2,200(17X2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 44.74%。

訪談重點

全球連接線束解決方案領導者 
貿聯成立於 1996 年，總部位於加州矽谷，註冊於開曼群島，為全球連接線
束解決方案的領導廠商。產品分為工業用、電器用、車用、資訊科技與其他
五大產線。主要應用於資訊科技用線、電腦週邊設備、數據中心用線、消費
電子用線、電器用線、車用線、醫療設備用線、光通訊設備、太陽能連接裝
置、工業設備用線、半導體設備等線材、連接器等領域。公司 26Q1 產品營
收比重為: 工業用 31%、電器用 12%、車用 8%、資訊科技 48%。

HPC 與半導體設備雙引擎驅動營益率創歷史新高 
26Q3 公司營收為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6%、YoY+51.3%，細分產品組合
來看，HPC 高效能運算受惠於 AI 基礎設施持續投資，其中電源互連因高電
流與機櫃級配電需求強勁、旗下 XFS光學業務月營收運行率自年初以來已超
過翻倍、SPE半導體生產設備受惠於沉積與蝕刻等設備平台滲透率及次系統
承接比重提升，並持續大幅優於整體 WFE 市場、其他業務整體市場環境好
壞參半，但電氣化與自動化需求持續推進，另併購之 Interplex於 26Q3 僅計
入 7天營收，10月起完整併表並單月貢獻 6,700萬美元；毛利率為 32.6%，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，搭配營業費用率降至歷史新低，帶動營業利益
率創下歷史新高；稅後淨利 42.78 億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；稅後
EPS 21.93元。

動能從單一零組件邁向子系統級解決方案，動能延續至 2027年後 
工業用業務方面，受惠工廠自動化需求活動持續改善，加上受惠於電氣化與
基礎設施現代化的能源、電動車及特用工程應用穩健推進，帶動整體表現持
續成長；此外，公司在機器人、航太、海事、醫療科技、半導體量測及儲能
等領域的客製化線束與整合方案，部分已成為穩定貢獻來源，部分則處於早
期認證與商業開發階段。

半導體生產設備方面，主要受惠於沉積與蝕刻設備平台滲透率與內含價值提
升，且客戶針對高複雜度製程的投資預期強勁並延伸至 2030 年以後，帶動
高複雜度組裝、子系統製造與整合、測試及機箱組裝的委外需求持續擴大。
隨著各產業客戶對供應商的要求從單一零組件轉向具備工程支援、產品認

評等分級：買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）； 逢低買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）； 區間

- 3 - 
訪談速報 
證、量產準備度與穩定執行力的子系統級解決方案，結合併購 Interplex所帶
來的精密機構與機殼整合綜效，相關成長動能預期將延續至 2027 年及更長
遠的基礎設施與產能擴張週期。

機櫃內銅纜、機櫃外光纖並進，提供完整互連方案 
公司持續受惠於高速銅互連解決方案在支援更高頻寬與維持訊號完整性上的
優勢；隨著 AI系統頻寬需求、光纖密度與互連複雜度提高，光互連基礎設施
的重要性持續提升，同時銅互連在機櫃內短距離傳輸、成本與部署特性上仍
具效率優勢，因此光互連不會單向取代銅互連，而是機櫃內以銅纜為主、機
櫃外長距離傳輸以光纖為主，兩者將依頻寬、傳輸距離、功耗與封裝架構不
同而長期共存。公司透過旗下 XFS Communications 補強精密光學組裝、連
接器化製程、高密度封裝與快速量產擴張能力，並陸續於 OFC、Computex
與 OCP展出光纖方案、CPO交換器托盤及 12.8T XPO收發方案，同時推進
次世代基於 Micro-VCSEL的機櫃內擴展（Scale-up）光學互連技術與外部技
術合作，成為後續 HPC業務擴張的重要動能。

在 HPC端，AI基礎設施進入長期投資與升級週期，隨著算力密度持續攀升，
資料中心的物理基礎設施挑戰正延伸至高電流電力傳輸、散熱及系統級整
合。公司產品線已從單一電源線延伸至 Busbars、Power Whips、連接器、
匯流排槽以及水冷板、分歧管、液冷快接頭與漏液偵測等全液冷 AI 機櫃方
案，並結合併購 Interplex的精密機構與機殼整合能力強化系統級競爭力；至
於低壓直流與高壓直流新電源架構的導入管理層指出將取決於各家客戶的系
統設計、產品認證進度及資料中心電力基礎設施的就緒程度，目前公司正與
客戶密切共同開發次世代與下下世代架構，以高度彈性配合其部署排程。

新世代平台放量帶動獲利爆發 
預估 26Q4營收 339.62億元，QoQ+21.9%、YoY+71.7%，因為工業用季節
性需求回溫，以及 HPC 新平台所帶動；毛利率 32.8%，QoQ+0.2ppts、
YoY+0.9ppts，因新平台導入，因此壓抑毛利率成長幅度；稅後淨利 51.7億
元，QoQ+20.9%、YoY+88.7%；稅後 EPS 26.5元。

預估 2026年營收 1,059.61億元，YoY+48.7%，持續受惠於資料中心業務；
毛利率 31.5%，YoY-0.2ppts；稅後淨利 147.04 億元，YoY+63.3%；稅後
EPS 75.37元。

預估 2027年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於新世代平台放量；毛
利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，
YoY+74.6%；稅後 EPS 131.58元。

4576 大銀微(HMC)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研 究 員：洪 國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
 在半導體先進封裝、AI 及矽光子等高階應用需求暢旺，其中
微米與奈米級定位系統交期 5~6 個月，精密運動及控制元件交
期約 3~4 個月，呈現供不應求。 
 受惠於晶圓大廠持續擴充先進製程、後段設備廠訂單持續成
長，大銀微在超高精度平台訂單已經看到 2027 年年中，另外
搭配新產能將於 2Q27 投產。預估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位
系統營收金額 31.5 億元，YoY+28.0%。 
一. 公告 3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.14 元 
大銀微 3Q26 營收金額 12.18 億元，YoY+76.73%，QoQ+19.01%，
營業利益 2.96 億元，YoY+312.6%，QoQ+61.12%，稅後盈餘 2.57
億元，YoY+255.8%，QoQ+123.72%，稅後 EPS 2.14 元。 
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.44 元 
大銀微 10/2026 營收金額 4.10 億元，YoY+68.75%，MoM-3.96%。
在訂單呈現滿載水準，預估大銀微 4Q26 營收金額 12.84 億元，
YoY+69.94% ， QoQ+5.37% ，預估營業利益 3.32 億元，
YoY+232.13% ， QoQ+12.21% ，預估稅後盈餘 2.92 億元，
YoY+223.25%，QoQ+13.78%，預估稅後 EPS 2.44 元。 
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 6.50 元 
大銀微 2026 年在半導體、PCB 與矽光子檢測裝置需求延續，帶
動微米奈米級高精度定位系統訂單維持數個月的能見度。另外中
國大陸工具機谷底復甦，推升控制元件產品需求，帶動大銀微 2026
年營運成長。預估大銀微 2026 年營收 43.8 億元，YoY+61.37%，
預估稅後盈餘 7.79 億元，YoY+222.63%，預估稅後 EPS 6.50 元。 
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 9.00 元 
大銀微 2027 年在推動的 One HIWIN Solution 單一零組件供應
商，往次系統、完整定位平台以及共同開發解決方案的角色移動，
搭配同集團上銀資源整合，將有助於大銀微產品附加價值提升。
宏遠預估大銀微 2027 年營收金額 53.00 億元，YoY+21.00%，預
估稅後盈餘 10.79 億元，YoY+38.52%，預估稅後 EPS 9.00 元。 
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 252 元 
觀察國內傳動元件同業 2027 年營收成長率亞德客 ROE 21%，營
收成長率 10%，PER 15X ；上銀 ROE 11% ，營收成長率 16%，
PER 22X，大銀微 ROE 21%，營收成長率 21%，PER 21.6X，以
ROE 與營收成長率相較同業來的佳，評價與同業相較仍在低檔水
準，故投資建議買進，目標價 252 元（28X 2027 年稅後 EPS）

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
大銀微系統股份有限公司創立於 1997 年 4 月，資本額 11.98 億
元，並於 2019 年 9 月正式掛牌上市。大銀微行屬於上銀集團旗
下的核心關係企業。大銀微聚焦精準定位與高性能驅動控制技
術，並以機電整合、產品製造及技術服務之整體能力作為核心競
爭優勢，深耕半導體、電子生產設備、智慧自動化及工具機等領
域。受惠半導體先進製程與封裝需求成長，大銀微以領先業界之
精密定位技術及系統整合方案擴大服務範圍，針對客戶需求提供
專業精密定位系統整合方案，透過技術互補與研發合作，協助全 
球頂尖半導體客戶縮短開發週期並提升產出效益。

二. 2026 年營運概況及 2027 年展望 
大銀行營收主要有精密運動及控制元件、微米與奈米級定位系統
與其他三部分。

預估 2027 年精密運動及控制元件營收金額 21.00 億元，
YoY+11.7%

精密運動及控制元件產品線包括：線性馬達、直驅馬達、真空馬
達、力矩馬達、伺服馬達、高速主軸馬達、位置量測系統等，並
搭配自行開發的驅動器及控制器，達奈米級高精度定位控制功
能。廣泛應用於半導體設備/自動化搬送/FPD 設備/PCB 設備
/AOI 檢測設備/SMT 設備/雷射加工器/各式機器手臂/工具機/電
動車相關/太陽能等不同產業。精密運動及控制元件 2025 年營收
金額 12.64 億元，YoY+3.1%。

精密運動及控制元件 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 13.83 億元，
YoY+45.43%。在中國大陸對驅動器需求高，目前處於供不應求狀
態，帶動包括線性馬達、直驅馬達、伺服馬達產能稼動率在高檔
水準，預估 2026 年精密運動及控制元件產品營收金額 18.80 億
元，YoY+48.7%。

預期 2027 年，在中國大陸工具機出口額與金屬切削設備產業仍呈
現正向發展，整體需求大於供給，訂單能見度己達 3~4 個月，預
估 2027 年精密運動及控制元件產品營收金額 21.00 億元，
YoY+11.7%。

預估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位系統營收金額 31.5 億元，
YoY+28.0%

微米與奈米級定位系統產品線包括：各式高精密定位平台，如單
軸、X-Y 雙軸、橋式、龍門、空氣軸承、多維度定位平台，可滿
足各種產業應用的需求，如自動化設備、汽車產業、光學檢測、
雷射加工、膠材塗佈、金屬加工等。微米與奈米級定位系統 2025
年營收金額 14.27 億元，YoY+41.8%。

微米與奈米級定位系統 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 16.8 億元，
YoY+69.5%。在 AI 產業熱潮帶動半導體產業投資本支出持續加
速，間接推升微米與奈米級定位系統需求，其中半導體定位平台
能見度約 5~6 個月，超高精度平台甚至可看到 2027 年中，在此
熱潮帶動下，預估 2026 年微米與奈米級定位系統營收金額 24.60
億元，YoY+72.4%。

預期 2027 年，受惠於晶圓大廠持續擴充先進製程、後段設備廠訂
單持續成長，大銀微在超高精度平台訂單已經看到 2027 年年中，
包括 CoWoP、CoPoS、FoPLP 都持續配合客戶打樣中，另外搭
配新產能將於 2Q27 投 產。預 估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位系統營
收金額 31.5 億元，YoY+28.0%。

## Document 234: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

### Page 8

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

## Document 304: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause, however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downside 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widely 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is 
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals”. By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutinised. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike. But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, the decision is justified by the need to further analyse risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that any rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United States). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the HICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1%-2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

## Document 305: Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Chipping in: how chips have  
become a huge driver of the dollar

1 
Authors 
Mallika Sachdeva 
Strategist

November 16, 2026 
What do chips have to do with a new era of dollar dominance? As we discuss in 
this paper – a great deal. Chips are not only critical to the future of AI but have 
recently emerged as a huge driver of global trade and capital flows. 
It is well known that the US is dominant in upstream chip technology and 
downstream compute. This note delves deeper into the workings of the middle of 
the chain where flows have been reinforcing the dollar’s global role. The 
manufacturing of chips largely takes place in Asia where chip-related trade 
surpluses have ballooned. As we show, these surpluses are finding their way 
back to the US via investments in US capital markets. 
Asian chip surpluses are not being recycled into US Treasuries like petrodollar 
surpluses were, but into US equities. Foreign inflows into US equities have 
crossed USD1tn a year and become the mainstay of US deficit funding. The 
biggest source of increase over the past year has come from Asia. 
Equity flows have not just been supporting the US dollar but have provided 
financing to the companies driving the AI buildout and chips boom itself. This 
introduces forms of circularity and potential instability to global flows. Moreover, 
equities are a much riskier asset class than bonds – US stocks are three times as 
volatile as Treasuries, with tech stocks up to ten times as much. Growing dollar 
dependence on inherently riskier capital should spill into higher US dollar 
volatility over time. 
The rise of chips as a driver of dollar dominance can be seen as a symptom of 
change in the ultimate source of US power - from geopolitical to technological 
leadership. This power is increasingly concentrated in the private sector with 
dollar supportive flows intermediated via private balance sheets. 
Understanding this new framework and its risks is even more important given 
rapid shifts in news flow around AI, from the US political climate, high-stakes 
capital market raisings, potential tightening in export controls, and the highly 
consequential technology race with China.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

The rise of the chip dollar 
For the first time in modern history, global trade in semiconductors now 
exceeds trade in oil and gas (Figure 1) based on the four quarters of global 
exports till Q2-2026. More relevant from a currency perspective, is that chip 
surpluses in Asia ex-China have caught up to petroleum-related surpluses in the 
Gulf (Figure 2).  Why does this matter and what does this reflect?

Figure 1:  Global trade in chips has overtaken trade in 
oil & gas for the first time in history

Figure 2:  Asia (ex-China)’s chip surpluses are now as 
large as the Gulf’s petroleum-related surpluses

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS Codes 2709, 
2710, 2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS 2709, 2710, 
2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542; Asia ex-China is Japan, Korea, Taiwan & ASEAN

We argue that the petrodollar is – rather quickly - being superseded by the chip-
dollar. This is both literal in terms of global surplus accumulation, but also 
archetypal. In other words, the petrodollar and chip-dollar are archetypes for two 
different states of the world, and for alternative foundations of dollar dominance.  
The petrodollar regime can be understood as one where US geopolitical 
leadership – over global security, international rules, and free trade – motivated 
global savings to be reinvested in the US sovereign via Treasury holdings. This 
cheapened funding for the US government which in turn supported the financing 
of US public goods provision.  
The emerging chip-dollar regime is one where US technology leadership is 
motivating global savings to be reinvested in US companies – providing funding 
for the US corporate sector to make enormous investments in new technology 
such as AI.  
The transition from the petrodollar to the chip-dollar is therefore a 
manifestation of change in the US’ leadership style - from geopolitical to 
technology leadership. This is not only changing the nature of global imbalances 
and capital flow recycling but is reshaping the foundations of dollar dominance.  
Revisiting the petrodollar regime  
After the 1971 Nixon shock, when the dollar’s convertibility to gold was 
terminated, we moved into a fully fiat currency world. The US dollar since then 
has been backed only by the fiscal credibility of the US government and the 
willingness of the world to finance US deficits by buying US assets.  
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

The 1974 petrodollar deal provided a crucial pillar of support for the dollar in its 
fiat infancy and sowed the seeds for a post-Bretton Woods regime in which US 
geopolitical leadership supported dollar dominance.  In 1974, in exchange for US 
security and military guarantees, Saudi Arabia agreed to price and invoice oil sales 
in USD. These USD denominated oil surpluses were then reinvested in US 
Treasuries. This was soon replicated across the Gulf. The 1990s and 2000s saw 
huge growth in commodity and manufacturing surpluses around the world. These 
were arguably supported by the US’s global backstop for trade and security – with 
surpluses saved in USD and recycled into US Treasuries via public balance sheets, 
in what can be considered a petrodollar-plus regime.  
We wrote earlier in the year how the Iran war could undermine the critical 
foundations of the petrodollar system by challenging the US security umbrella 
and shifting perceptions around US geopolitical leadership. We maintain that 
these changes are underway. But what has become apparent since then is that 
something else has been rising to take the petrodollar’s place in support of the 
dollar.  
The chip dollar is working differently  
While petrodollar surpluses were recycled into US Treasuries, chips surpluses 
are being recycled very differently.  
We focus on the chip surpluses being built in Asia ex-China. We note that China 
runs a very large semiconductor deficit, despite having the world’s largest overall 
trade surplus - something we will return to later. Asia’s chip surpluses are thus 
primarily hosted in Korea and Taiwan which are large semiconductor 
manufacturers, to a lesser extent in Japan which produces key materials, and 
ASEAN-10 which plays a role in assembly, packaging and testing.  
To explore how chip dollar surpluses are being recycled, we zoom in on South 
Korea – the epitome of the boom in chips trade.  
Korea’s semiconductor exports have grown over 200% YoY this year, with Korean 
chipmakers dominating in the manufacture of memory chips critical to AI 
acceleration. As Figure 3 shows, Korea’s semiconductor trade surplus has 
ballooned to close to USD250bn with 100% of Korea’s overall trade surplus now 
on account of chips alone.  
What is striking is that Korea’s chip surplus is being recycled abroad entirely via 
equities. Foreign sales of Korean equities have accounted for half the capital 
outflows in the past year, while Korean purchases of overseas equities account 
for the other half (Figure 3). Korean investments in overseas equities have been 
five times more than Korean purchases of overseas bonds in the last two years. In 
short, chip surpluses are being saved in global equities, not debt. 
The primary destination of Korea’s overseas equity purchases has been the US. 
While the US accounts for around 50% of global equity market capitalization, we 
note it is 70% of Korea’s overseas equity holdings (IMF CPIS). The US bias is even 
more exaggerated for Korean retail investors – a group empowered by access to 
financial technology. Korean households have 90% of their equity exposure in the 
US (Figure 4). It appears that North Asian capital is flowing back to the very US 
technology companies that are driving the chip surpluses themselves.

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

Figure 3:  Asian chip surpluses are being recycled into 
US equities – not US Treasuries like petrodollars were

Figure 4: 70% of Korea’s equity exposure abroad is to 
the US, and that figure is more than 90% for households

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, CEIC; Note: The semiconductor trade balance here is 
measured using Korea’s Customs Service classification for Semiconductors and 
equipment for semiconductor manufacturing

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics; Note: Data based on IMF’s 
Coordinated Portfolio Investment Survey as of end-2025

The reach for US equities by Asian retail is not just happening in Korea. In Japan, 
there have been significant allocations to US focused ETFs, encouraged by the 
NISA scheme. And as we have argued in our earlier paper, the US’s embrace of 
asset tokenization and the upcoming shift by US exchanges to 23/5 trading could 
further embolden and enable retail investors to access US equities. We could thus 
just be at the beginning of this new trend. Countries in North Asia like Korea and 
Japan not only have large savings pools, but also open capital accounts.  
This shift has shown up dramatically on the US capital account. Foreign 
purchases of US equities have exceeded USD1tn over the past year (Figure 5). 
Interestingly, most of the increase in purchases has come from Asia (Figure 6). 
Notably, foreign purchases of US equities are now more than double US Treasury 
allocations, which appear in relative decline. Even foreign purchases of US 
corporate bonds now exceed US Treasuries. This is notable considering the ramp 
up in hyper scaler issuance.

Figure 5: Foreign flows into US stocks now exceed 
flows into US Treasuries by a significant margin

Figure 6: Most of the increase in US equity purchases 
over the past year has come from Asia

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Chips are the oil of the new economy  
The power of having oil priced and paid for in dollars was not just the recycling 
of petrodollar surpluses, but the value chain impact of having a key upstream 
resource dollarized. Oil is a critical input to almost every industrial process via 
petrochemicals – it is found in everything from plastic to paints, tires to 
cosmetics. It also plays a role in moving traded goods around the world as the 
transport fuel for ships. We would argue that having oil priced in dollars played a 
role in dollarizing global trade as companies looked to match the currency of 
downstream receivables to the upstream costs of oil. This is consistent with the 
Dominant Currency Paradigm (Gopinath et al, 2020).  
Chips arguably play a similar role in the digital and consumer economy as oil has 
played in the industrial economy. Chips are inputs to everything from consumer 
electronics, to automobiles, computers, communication devices, and most 
recently the bedrock of modern AI systems.  
Indeed, chips make up the lion’s share of spending in the AI buildout. Analysis by 
Epoch AI found that servers represented about 60% of the annualized cost of an 
indicative 1GW data centre in the US (Figure 7). According to the Semiconductor 
Industry Association (SIA), chips represent 95% of an AI server rack's value. In 
simple terms therefore, we can say chips make up over 50% of total capital 
expenditures for an AI data centre. Bloomberg Intelligence estimates hyper-
scaler capital expenditure commitments of close to USD8tn out to 2031 (Figure 
8), which would suggest a further USD4tn of chips demand from AI alone.

Figure 7: More than half the cost of the AI data centre 
build out is going towards chips

Figure 8:  Capital expenditure in the AI buildout may 
still have a long way to go

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Epoch AI; * Note: Based on a stylized model of a US 
hyperscaler AI data center that has 1 GW capacity. CapEx is converted to annual costs 
over each asset’s lifespan, assuming 5 years for IT equipment like chips.

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

To be sure, the market will remain sensitive to shifts in news flow and risks that 
the AI data centre buildout might slow whether due to the changing political 
climate in the US, calls for growing public oversight and safety monitoring of the 
industry, or a renewed focus on export controls.  
There are also open questions as to whether the boom in chips demand will be 
“one-off” as capacity is built or will be ongoing like with oil. Oil is a huge source of 
ongoing dollar flows primarily because of how consistent and trade-intensive it 
16 November 2026
 FX Special Report

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

is: 100 million barrels are consumed daily with 70% of them being traded. We note 
that the replacement rate of chips in an average data centre is estimated at 3-7 
years, which suggests there could be ongoing demand even once the projected 
capex buildout reaches its zenith. But it remains to be seen whether the chips 
dollar can generate the type of enduring and stable recycling that the petrodollar 
has generated. 
Staying with the comparison between the petrodollar and chip-dollar, arguably it 
was US military presence in the world’s largest oil producing region in the 1970s 
– the Gulf - that gave the US control over the pricing of oil. What is it that gives 
the US control over the pricing and trade in chips? 
US control over chips: technology, economic rents, and supply chains   
The integrated circuit was invented in the US in 1958 with the microchip industry 
flourishing in California to become the well-known Silicon Valley. It is hard to 
overstate the continued dominance of the US in chips since then. Work by Sean 
Kenji Starrs of Kings College London maps the present-day dominance in 
compelling terms1. 
While most chip manufacturing happens in Asia – which is where the trade 
surpluses discussed above are being accumulated – most of the economic rents 
to chip-making accrues to US companies. Around 50% of the value in the 
semiconductor supply chain is in the design process where US companies control 
87% of the revenue (Starrs). The US has a near monopoly on the electronic design 
automation (EDA) software necessary for design, and controls around 60% of the 
revenue in semiconductor manufacturing equipment without which chips cannot 
be made. Materials, manufacturing, packaging and testing of chips happen 
outside the US, but these are worth only around 30% of the estimated value of 
the industry. Indeed, Starrs finds that 70% of the profits from the semiconductor 
industry flow to US firms (Figure 9).  
Furthermore, we note that all key countries involved in the supply chain of 
advanced chips remain US aligned. Japan plays an important role in materials, 
while Taiwan and Korea dominate in production (foundries). Importantly each of 
these North Asian players leans geopolitically towards the US over China. The US 
has arguably been less vocal about withdrawing economic support for security in 
North Asia than with NATO. One can thus posit the existence of a US-Asia chip-
dollar bloc. We note that the Netherlands has a monopoly on EUV lithography 
machines required to make the most advanced chips, but production of these 
machines in turn have a dependency on a US optical equipment firm helping 
grant the US crucial leverage even here.  
The potent combination of US control over key upstream technologies and 
intellectual property, alongside a supply chain located in US allies has given the 
US enormous control over chips. In recent years, the US has actively exercised 
this power via export controls to limit China’s access to US advanced chips and 
technologies. This has been the subject of books from Chip War by Chris Miller to 
Chokepoints by Edward Fishman. The importance of ensuring the integrity of US 
export controls was made apparent in recent comments by US Trade 
Representative Greer who noted that the US wants the “crown jewels” of the 
semiconductor industry to remain in the hands of the US or its allies. The US

1 Starrs, Sean Kenji. 2025. “Ringfencing Globalization: US Structural Power, Techno-Nationalism & Chinese Dependent 
Integration.” In Chapter 8 in Globalization in a Turbulent Era 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

government has arguably had significant influence over the private sector sale of 
US chips technology and may double down on this after recent events at Nvidia 
to limit both direct access to US chips and the leasing of global data centers built 
with US chips to non-US allies (CNBC).

Figure 9: The US is very dominant in chips – controlling

Where does China fit in with all this?  
This note has looked at key changes in the industrial drivers of global imbalances 
(from fossil fuels to chips) and capital flows to the US (from debt to equities). 
Where does China – with the world’s biggest trade surplus – fit in?  
China now runs an over USD2 trillion manufactured goods surplus per year but 
continues to run a persistent semiconductor deficit (Figure 10). While US export 
controls limit access to the most advanced computing chips, China continues to 
import large volumes of memory and logic chips. Restrictions on access to 
advanced manufacturing equipment, particularly EUV lithography, have 
constrained China’s advances in domestic chip-making at the technological 
frontier, although greater self-sufficiency remains a major strategic priority. We 
note that the other drag on China’s trade position is commodities, mostly fossil 
fuels. Notably therefore, the remaining holes in China’s trade surplus are 
dominated by chip-dollar and petrodollar imports. In a forthcoming paper, we will 
look at China’s gains in renewable energy, new energy exports, and electrification, 
examining how these could emerge as long-term sources of power in both the AI 
race, and in increasing China’s independence from the dollar.  
The recycling of China’s manufactured goods surplus remains the topic of 
considerable debate. China’s surplus is in many ways the mirror of the US deficit, 
albeit with growing contribution from Europe, and increasingly intermediated 
through the Global South. There remains a school of thought that these Chinese 
savings find their way to the US through state banks and custodian balance 
sheets. But we note important points of difference with the chip dollar recycling 
we have discussed.  Nearly 50% of the net international assets held by Korea, 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 8

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Japan and Taiwan are in portfolio investments – namely equities and bonds. For 
China, this number is less than 20%. This is consistent with China’s capital account 
restrictions on private capital, as well as a growing geopolitical preference to limit 
exposure to US capital markets. Instead, China holds a much greater share of 
direct and other assets – reflecting growth in Chinese state-directed lending and 
acquisition of hard assets particularly in the Global South. On public reserve 
balance sheets, we note a growing preference for gold over dollar assets. Finally, 
we note a shift towards conversion of China’s surpluses, showing up in a stronger 
RMB and lower domestic rates.  
Bringing it together 
In the age of AI, it is well known that the US is dominant in both upstream chip 
technology as well as downstream computing. The US designs and controls 
supply chains for the world’s most advanced chips and is home to 10 times as 
many data centers as any other country (Stanford HAI). Both are sources of US 
power. This note has delved deeper into the workings of the middle of the chain 
where new flows are reinforcing the dollar’s global role in trade and capital flows. 
The manufacturing of high-end chips takes place in Asia ex-China and generates 
large trade surpluses there. This money is finding its way back to the US via 
Asian investments in US equities and corporate bonds. These capital flows are 
both funding the US trade deficit and providing financing to US companies that 
are driving the AI boom. Chips are thus ensuring the continued dominance of 
the dollar in global trade, payments, capital markets, and private sector savings, 
as well as supporting the US AI buildout seen as crucial to the future.  
There is an element of circularity to be conscious of: equity flows and financing 
for US AI related stocks are being funded out of Asian chip-related surpluses 
which are themselves being boosted by demand from US AI companies. This 
could introduce instability in global flows should demand be reassessed. For 
instance, if consumer demand for compute does not keep up with costly 
investments in supply, this would not just reduce earnings estimates for AI 
companies, but ultimately chip demand. One can see how this would challenge 
foreign equity financing for the US from both a push and pull perspective.  
Separately, as we noted in our last paper, proliferation of Chinese open-source 
models could also undermine the profitability of US companies and the 
attractiveness of US markets to global capital.  
The fact that the US dollar has become more dependent on foreign equity 
inflows than US Treasuries should make it inherently more volatile and exposed. 
Equities by nature are a riskier asset class than bonds. Recent realized volatility in 
S&P500 has been 3 times as much as in a US 10Y Treasuries. NASDAQ volatility is 
double that of the S&P and the Philly Semiconductor Index more than triple.  That 
US deficit financing is now dependent on capital flows into a more volatile asset 
class should spill over into currency volatility in time.  
In sum, US dollar dominance is now dependent on US technology leadership 
driven by private companies, and the flows underpinning the dollar are 
increasingly being intermediated on private sector balance sheets. A dollar driven 
by chips, AI and private capital connections should be inherently riskier.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas

Companies subject to collective proceedings (receivership and judicial liquidation). Monthly data from the
Banque de France aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects
Business creations Insee Monthly
Survey of business creations (all types, companies, sole traders, micro-entrepreneurs). Monthly data from
INSEE aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects

Non-performing loans ratio EBA Quarterly
Gross non performing loans and advances at amortised cost / Total gross loans and advances at
amortised cost
Share of underperforming loans (Stage 2 IFRS 9) ECB Quarterly
Loans and advances in Stage 2 / Total loans and advances. Loans and advances at amortised cost and at
fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies Banque de France Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.
Share of 90+ days past due payments Urssaf Monthly Contributions still to be collected by Urssaf / Amount of contributions due to Urssaf

## Document 349: Global Markets Outlook 2027_ Growing Pains

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Markets Outlook 2027_ Growing Pains.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

### Page 9

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our
